  Dick's passing is a great loss to us all and to me personally. I
  have been privileged to count him among my friends. Dick was
  very warm, (contrary to what some of you who debated him may
  have experienced on the List) intelligent, funny and a most
  engaging human being. He always stood by me, at crucial times
  coming to my defense and he was always there with the right
  words to encourage me when I needed to hear them most. I will
  miss him, his always unwavering support and most of all, his
  friendship. I am very deeply saddened.
  If we dream after we die, I hope Dick finds himself standing
  down by the wire at a thoroughbred racetrack, surrounded by old
  friends and loved ones, at a place where the sun always shines,
  the horses are all champions, and where every nag he picks wins
  by a nose every time.

  So long old friend... you'll be missed.
  John Velez

A Tribute To Richard H. Hall




                                                           UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Jul > Jul 18

                                                     A Tribute To Richard H. Hall
  From: Larry W. Bryant <overtci.nul>
  Date: Fri, 17 Jul 2009 20:22:25 -0400
  Archived: Sat, 18 Jul 2009 09:35:00 -0400
  Subject: A Tribute To Richard H. Hall

  A phone call to me this afternoon (July 17, 2009) from UFO
  researcher Dan Pinchas of Germantown, Md., brought news of the
  death of one of ufology's giants: Richard H. Hall, 78, of
  Brentwood, Md.
  For at least a year, I'd known that Dick had been recovering
  from colon-cancer surgery, but he counseled me not to spread
  that knowledge. By e-mail several months ago, he'd told me he
  was doing okay. Now, I learn that he'd been undergoing
  chemotherapy. He apparently died in his sleep this morning.
  Seemingly right up to his last moments, Dick was pursuing his
  ufological calling, for it's been only several weeks since my
  receipt of the latest issue of his bimonthly newsletter Journal
  of UFO History.
  My association and friendship with this consummate scholar date
  back to November 1957, when I met him for the first time at the
  Washington, D. C., headquarters of the now-defunct National
  Investigations Committee on Aerial Phenomena (NICAP). There, as
  I sauntered into the front office, I spied him with his head
  down and eyes focused on a piece of correspondence he intently
  was typing upon a manual typewriter.
  Eventually, Dick became a role model for my own baby steps as a
  UFO researcher-activist. He not only wrote well and fluently; he
  also had a keen hand at line drawing. Noted mostly for his
  seminal two-volume work The UFO Evidence, he also had edited the
  NICAP newsletter (The UFO Investigator) and some of the
  organization's special reports. His two books on the subject
  matter (The Challenge of Unidentified Flying Objects (with Prof.
  Charles A. Maney - 1961); and Uninvited Guests (1988)) remain
  collector's items.
  His other major contributions to the field derived from his
  service on the board of directors for the Fund for UFO Research,
  Inc. At separate periods, he also served as editor of the
  monthly journal of the Mutual UFO Network, Inc., and as a
  columnist for the monthly newsstand periodical UFO Magazine.
  (See my essay about his career at this blog's Item 3.2.)
  Besides his UFOlit products, Dick managed to publish a small
  handbook on raising house plants, plus an overview of the role
  of women in America's civil war.
  Dick Hall's commitment to serious UFO research and his high
  standard of personal achievement in the field began during his
  student days at Tulane University, where he majored in
  philosophy. I take delight in possessing copies of his self-
  published newsletter from that early period - the UFO Critical
  Bulletin. Critical thinking (and action), you see, became the
  hallmark of his scholarship. If anyone deserved an honorary
  doctorate in ufology, it certainly was he.
  I never could've asked for a better mentor, friend, and
  colleague in this field. Thank you, again, Sir Richard, for the
  enriching part you played in this long journey of inquiry,
  networking, and enlightenment.



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/jul/m18-006.shtml[11/12/2011 11:16:25]
A Tribute To Richard H. Hall

Dick Hall's Passing




                                                           UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Jul > Jul 18

                                                              Dick Hall's Passing
  From: Ray Stanford <dinosaurtracker.nul>
  Date: Fri, 17 Jul 2009 20:46:50 -0400
  Archived: Sat, 18 Jul 2009 09:38:00 -0400
  Subject: Dick Hall's Passing

  Although Dick and I had our conflicts, I was, quite aside from
  that, actually fond of him (our corresponding went way back into
  the 1950s) and very highly regard his many invaluable
  contributions to Ufology. Sheila (my wife) and I were quite
  proud of him when he quit making contributions to UFO Magazine
  and, de facto, told them where to stuff their smorgasbord of
  rumors and tales told in the night.
  Of course, we missed Dick's intelligent, discerning articles
  there, but sometimes it is wise to rise above UFOlogical mire,
  and in withdrawing from the magazine, he was doing what I think
  was the right thing for him in that circumstance. Others might
  disagree, but we must always be aware of how many persons think
  in terms of "Birds of a feather flock together...", without
  their recognizing a distinctly individual avian in a seeming
  flock, within which simile Dick would stand out, as might a
  Scarlet Ibis in distinct contrast to a flock of cuckoos.
  Dick was the very first person Sheila and I invited out to
  dinner after I moved up here to Maryland in early February,
  1986. We both enjoyed the conversations with him that night and
  during the several times we invited him over to the house to
  discuss various UFOlogical matters. We loved it when, one night,
  Dick declared, "Ray, you are as mysterious as UFOs themselves!"
  I have always loved Paul Gaugain's painting called Soyez
  Mysterieux! (Be Mysterious) - especially its title - and so got
  a kick out of his comment more than Dick ever realized or even
  intended. He might not have intended it as an actual compliment,
  but I chose to accept it in that way.
  Ufology has sustained a real loss with Dick's passing. It will
  never be quite the same. He will be deeply missed, even by a
  person like me who had conflicts with him. Let me make this
  clear, though: Dick and I had many areas of agreement in things
  UFOlogical, and I will miss his highly intelligent and
  discerning insights into UFO-related matters that could confound
  the 'Johnny-Come-Latelys' in this field of study and
  controversy. He was one of the few remaining of us who, IMHO,
  lived within what I regard as the real Ufology, seeking to
  understand real objects being reported and the puzzling meanings
  that might exist behind their seemingly rather covert
  operations, instead of slogging around in the current morass of
  paranoid rumors and human fantasies.
  Believing as I do in survival of bodily death, I pray that Dick
  shall find release and deep peace, a strengthening rest upon the
  winds of time, before his longing for physical-life experience
  draws him into another human womb. I know he must have suffered
  terribly in his last weeks or months, and am glad he has moved
  beyond that.
  May Dick behold in light, wonders for which, from here, we only
  grapple in relative darkness.

  Respectfully,
  Ray Stanford

                                                            Re: Richard Hall Dies
  From: Bill Chalker <bill_c.nul>
  Date: Sat, 18 Jul 2009 22:38:42 +1000
  Archived: Sat, 18 Jul 2009 10:40:00 -0400
  Subject: Re: Richard Hall Dies

  >From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
  >To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >Date: Fri, 17 Jul 2009 16:12:56 -0400
  >Subject: Richard Hall Dies
  >I have just received word that veteran ufologist Richard Hall,
  >who was both a friend and hero to me, died this morning of the
  >cancer he had been fighting.
  >Ufology has lost a giant.

  Richard Hall was a gentleman and true UFO scholar who championed
  to the end the causes of UFO history and scientific ufology. He
  achieved a remarkable record of scholarship which in part were
  bookended by his edited NICAP 1964 publication classic The UFO
  Evidence and its excellent sequel The UFO Evidence Volume II - A
  Thirty Year Report of 2001 which should be on every serious UFO
  scholar's & researcher's bookshelves.
  Dick was still contributing a great contribution with his
  Journal Of UFO History which he still managed to keep producing
  into his final year.
  On February 18 this year he emailed me, "I still have serious
  health problems and have had to cut back on many activities as a
  result, but I hope to continue the Journal as long as possible."
  The Journal and his long list of achievements are a remarkable
  legacy of a life well spent. They go beyond ufology, but I will
  limit comment to this field.
  It is appropriate that the final 'issue' he weighed into on this
  List was the cause of "scientific ufology" versus "popular
  ufology". It's an issue I feel strongly about as well.
  Good journey Dick, you have certainly left your mark with a
  great legacy rooted in solid research, a deep grasp of, and
  extraordinary involvement in, UFO history. His fight for a
  credible scientific ufology was also another constant in his
  legacy.
  He will be sorely missed.

  Bill Chalker
  http://theozfiles.blospot

  From: Joe McGonagle <joe.mcgonagle.nul>
  Date: Sat, 18 Jul 2009 14:26:48 +0100
  Archived: Sat, 18 Jul 2009 12:28:59 -0400
  Subject: Re: Richard Hall Dies

  Dick and I have had our disagreements, but I think we had more
  in common than is immediately obvious. The last on-List post
  that I could find from him is at:
  http://ufoupdateslist.com/2009/apr/m30-001.shtml
  It is a continuation of a thread which started out with us
  apparently both in opposition, but eventually finding the common
  ground.
  Dick deserves respect, whether or not you agree with his views.
  His efforts towards a 'scientific ufology' have been going on
  for decades, and I know that he was disappointed with the lack
  of uptake of his valuable reference "The UFO Evidence part II".
  For me, Dick was a benchmark to which the quality of other
  ufologists' output could be compared. Many fall well short of
  the standards he set.
  Bon Voyage, Dick.

  Joe

  Dear List Members,
  I would like to express my deep sadness after learning the death
  of Richard H. Hall.
  I had the privilege to meet, work and talk with Dick Hall
  between 1999 and 2007.
  Precisely during the Sign Historical Group UFO History Workshop
  circa may 1999 at O'Hare Chicago, Ill., organised by Thomas
  Tulien.
  I was at this time accompanying Jean-Jacques Velasco from
  CNES/SEPRA and Dominique Weinstein.
  We met again in Washington DC, during the National Press Club
  conference in november 2007 organised by James Fox and Leslie
  Kean.
  He was a Gentleman, and a researcher of High Class. Openminded,
  always helpful with a tremendous wealth of knowledge and
  documents about the UFO Phenomena.
  Dick Hall will be remembered worldwide by his active
  contribution to several TV documentaries made these last years
  in France and in the US.
  "Ovni: Le Secret Am=E9ricain (UFO: The American Secret)from
  Vincent Gi=E9ly aired on France 2) and more recently "I Saw
   What I Saw" (former "Beyond the Blue") from James C. Fox which
  will be the last tribute of his work achievement.


  Bernard L. Thouanel

  From: Peter Davenport <director.nul>
  Date: Sat, 18 Jul 2009 10:05:54 -0700
  Archived: Sat, 18 Jul 2009 14:21:08 -0400
  Subject: Re: Richard Hall Dies

  To List Members:
  I, too, was saddened to read the news of Dick's passing, and was
  equally saddened to learn of his chronic suffering from cancer.
  Given Dick's stellar work in ufology, it is particularly sad
  that he had to leave the fray, before seeing a resolution to his
  work.
  I knew of Dick, and of his work, for many decades before having
  my first opportunity to meet him. I had read The UFO Evidence,
  which alerted me to the quality of his research and writing, and
  which caused me to pay special attention to his contributions to
  this forum, as well as to others. I always read, and considered
  well, his comments, knowing that they were the product both of
  considerable experience, and cautious consideration.
  I think I first crossed paths with Dick at the convocation in
  Washington, D. C., hosted by Larry Landsman of the SciFi
  Channel. I was particularly impressed by Dick's apparent ability
  to sum up a point with a single sentence, a form of reticence I
  admire. In addition, I enjoyed his 'olde school' manner.
  His early departure leaves a high-water mark for all of us,
  and I believe it heaps even more work on the shoulders of those
  who pursue the UFO mystery in a serious-minded fashion.

  Peter Davenport
  NUFORC

Re: Brain Wiring Creates False Memories




                                                           UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Jul > Jul 18

                                           Re: Brain Wiring Creates False Memories
  From: Gerald O'Connell <goc.nul>
  Date: Thu, 16 Jul 2009 16:50:11 +0100
  Archived: Sat, 18 Jul 2009 16:04:50 -0400
  Subject: Re: Brain Wiring Creates False Memories

  >Source: New Scientist - Sutton, Surrey, England, UK
  >http://tinyurl.com/mav2kk
  >15 July 2009
  >Magazine issue 2716
  >Brain Wiring Creates False Memories
  >Brain connections that encourage the formation of false memories
  >have been identified. Such memories appear to be more likely in
  >people with high-quality links between neurons in a particular
  >brain area.
  >Individuals often recall the same events differently or report
  >memories of things they should have been too young to recall. To
  >find out if a tendency to manufacture false memories is
  >reflected in brain structure, Lluis Fuentemilla at the
  >University of Barcelona in Spain and colleagues induced them in
  >48 students in the lab.
  >The participants were asked to read four lists of 14
  >semantically related words, in which each list had an absent
  >"lure" word. For example, a list including "seat", "sofa",
  >"table" and "stool" had "chair" as its lure.
  >The students wrote down as many words as they could recall and
  >subsequently had their brains scanned. Those who "recalled" more
  >lures were assumed to be more prone to false memories, and were
  >found to have higher-quality neural connections, called axons,
  >in the superior longitudinal fascicle, known to be associated
  >with gist-based memory.
  >Journal of Neuroscience, DOI: 10.1523/jneurosci.5270-08.2009).
  >http://www.jneurosci.org/cgi/content/abstract/29/27/8698

  What goes unmentioned here is the critical difference between
  1.The processes involved in selectively or partially remembering
  individual items from a list;
  and
  2 The processes involved in creating a false memory involving a
  complex sequence of events.
  So before this is taken as some kind of supportive evidence for
  the idea that witnesses who 'remember' (whether through hypnotic
  regression or otherwise) events that are difficult to square
  with 'commonsense everyday material reality', let me make one
  important point: to extrapolate broadly from experimental
  results in connection with (1) to any kind of conclusions about
  what is involved in (2) constitutes a (regrettably common)
  subset of junk science - junk interpretation of data culled from
  solid science.



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/jul/m18-015.shtml[11/12/2011 11:16:29]
Re: Brain Wiring Creates False Memories

  The paper's findings would not be the first to suggest that
  semantic memory is connected with aspects brain structure.
  Unfortunately, we don't yet have comprehensive, definitive
  models for the operation of semantic memory, their variation in
  correlation with memory of complex events, and the way these
  distinctions might function in relation to brain structure. --

  Gerald O'Connell
  http://www.onlyport.com

  Hi Errol,
  I like this guy. I read - and still have - his book many years
  ago. His book is filled with humor and like this interview
  indicated, he takes himself less seriously than the PR jocks and
  the media painted him and the other astronauts. His remark is
  curious however. The precursor to this remark seems to eliminate
  what follows when he states "...radio contact with the Earth
  abruptly cuts off at the instant I disappear behind the moon, I
  am alone now, truly alone, and absolutely isolated from any
  known life. I am it." I don't know what to make of it. The
  interviewer didn't pick up on the remark.
  On another NASA note, the video tapes discovered a couple of
  years ago; I'm less than impressed with whatever historical
  department NASA has or had. How in the heck could they have


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/jul/m18-018.shtml[11/12/2011 11:16:31]
Re: Odd Words From Apollo 11 Astronaut Collins

  forgotton about something as important as video of                        man's first
  step on the Moon?
  The jury-rigged arrangement they had to broadcast the event
  should have been actively followed up with gathering up the
  tapes in Australia and getting them transferred to a medium such
  as 35mm film and any other medium they could come up with. The
  trouble with magnetic tapes is that if they are left without
  being re-stacked [run forward and back on their reels]
  periodically the magnetic impulses on the tape 'print through'
  from one layer to the next which eventually will result in
  deterioration of the magnetic information that is complete.
  That, it appears, is what NASA is trying to overcome now.
  It is a major problem with archiving magnetically recorded
  material. If left to sit they will eventually destroy themselves
  plus the mylars and the polymer plastic construction of the
  tapes deteriorates as well. Even todays's digital recordings
  will have to be transfered to new mediums because right now the
  lifespan of a disk is about 45-50 years before the disk itself
  begins to breakdown.
  So far paper has been the longest lived medium for storing
  information that we know about, but even that has it's problems
  where historical texts are concerned due to mild acids in the
  water used to make paper. The older etched recordings on a disk
  such as 78 RPM records has lasted for years but again they went
  to plastic which will deteriorate even if properly stored in
  climatically controlled conditions and away from sunlight - the
  enemy of plastic.
  But what else has NASA lost? We tend to think of NASA - or we
  did before the Challenger accident - as one of the bureaucracies
  that worked but history indicates that the agency has its own
  foul-ups that surface ocassionally in front page news. And there
  is the ongoing battle over what some NASA video transmissions
  might or might not be showing us.

  Don Ledger

  I remind the observer that this Wellesian "alien invasion" or
  "abduction" incident is a matter entirely of humans putting the
  threatening spin on things as a device. Again, something humans
  did, apart from aliens. One can't blame aliens for behavior
  provoked by human beings.
  All that said, perhaps what _can_ be taken away from this still
  unfortunate and inconvenient or even misdirecting occurrence can
  be observations from the story itself"
   "Parent Governor Richard Webb, who was in school that day,
  commented that many children had reported that it was 'the best
  day in school, ever. ' Following the day, a few parents
  expressed concerns that some of the children had been upset by
  the apparent realism, and the headteacher has spoken with them
  personally."
  "Others praised the day for its community links, the imaginative
  approach and the huge element of 'fun'. One parent commented
  that the entire school 'buzzed with the excitement' and the fact
  that 'the wider community came together to set up a real once in
  a lifetime event for our children was just fantastic."
  See, school is not supposed to be engaging, inventive and
  effective at inspiring individuals to think out of the box and
  ask questions about their reality. "School" is to produce
  docile employees.

                                                The Shadow Industry Falling Apart?
  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  Date: Sun, 19 Jul 2009 13:06:41 EDT
  Archived: Sun, 19 Jul 2009 14:00:19 -0400
  Subject: The Shadow Industry Falling Apart?

  We may be getting more help from the public regarding UFOs and
  related technologies.
  I believe it's because of the economic crisis and feelings of
  betrayl the public has been feeling over the events of the past
  ten years. I've been told by friends, families, neighbors for
  years that they worked on back engineering and other projects
  regarding ET or as one put it, exotic foreign technology. Some
  didn't even know where the technology they were working on came
  from but after going to the UFO websites and reading many of the
  articles and accounts they came to some cognitions.
  Recently I got in contact with more people, first hand witnesses
  who will swear that their parents, spouses, worked on UFO
  related issues for the U.S. and more areas we're not even aware
  of. These people are coming forward now because they know we've
  got technologies that could do the world good and aren't seeing
  those technologies being applied. They feel lied to and betrayed
  and the majority of these people are conservative Republicans!
  They're really ticked off and I'm seeing more and more of them
  just upping information. This UFO thing is generations deep now
  and I'm seeing for a fact there's been a Shadow Industry of
  sorts going on for decades. People living right next door to us
  or close kin working on things we've no idea exist but I'm also
  seeing that shroud of secrecy fall. I think outsourcing may have
  helped in this as many tech folks have lost jobs too or their
  close kin lost jobs and the burden of supporting others is too
  much to bear.
  Things are happening, whether for the better or worse only time
  will tell. I doubt very much that the U.S. will disclose
  anything and judging by the latest story on how the Apollo 11
  original movies/video had been erased is an indicator of the
  Sgt. Bilko/F Troop shenanigans that probably pervade the
  government's secret UFO agenda.

  Best,
  Greg

  From: Nick Balaskas <nikolaos.nul>
  Date: Sun, 19 Jul 2009 15:32:16 -0400

  Subject: Re: Ergot?

  >From: Bruce Maccabee <brumac.nul>

  >Subject: Ergot?

  >abduction?
  >(Yes, I know what ergot is.                   If you don't, google....)

  Hi Bruce,
  I first came across this word, ergot, on pages 9, 9b and 26a in
  the First Edition of 'UFOs, MJ-12 And The Government - A Report
  oN Government Involvement In UFO Crash Retrievals' by reseachers
  Grant Cameron and T. Scott Crain, Jr. which was published in
  1991 by the Mutual UFO Network, Inc. (MUFON).
  In March 1984 UFO researcher William Steinman wrote to Dr. Eric
  Arthur Walker (President of Penn State University from 1956 to
  1970) about his involvement with UFO crashes and their occupants
  after another UFO researcher, Stanton Friedman, learned a year
  earlier that Dr. Walker, an electrical engineer and member of
  the Research and Development Board, had attended secret meetings
  in the early 1950s at Wright Field (WPAFB) together with other
  top scientists and engineers. These meetings concerned crashed
  flying saucers that were recovered by the U.S. Government...
  Steinman's letter was returned back to him with the following
  cryptic message typed at the end of his letter:
  STOP!DON'T TRY TO FIND ME I CAME
  ON THAT MACHINE I WILL LEAVE MAY
  15 ERGOT QUIET QUIET QUIET
  Replies by Dr. Walker to follow-up letters and statements of
  fact he made on phone conversations with Steinman and other UFO
  researchers, including Tom Mickus of Toronto, revealed some very
  disturbing and hard to believe things about UFOs and their
  occupants. None of the UFO researchers managed to convince Dr.
  Walker before he passed away (he died on February 17, 1995) that
  the public had a right to know about the alien presence on
  Earth.
  Dr. Walker did admit to being associated with MJ-12 and
  described the members of this secretive group as a "handful of
  elite".
  Investigations by Friedman, Steinman and other UFO researchers
  over the years have identified many later members of MJ-12 and
  insiders who have had first hand knowledge of UFOs (eg. Edward
  Teller; Henry Kissinger, etc.) or direct encounters with ETs,
  including alien abductions - Richard Nixon, etc.

  Nick Balaskas

  experiences have similarities to things recorded in werewolf
  scares, witch manias and the like, often claimed to have been
  hallucinatory delusions caused by ergot fungus poisoning from
  infected wheat grain.

  fungi and seeds but I don't suppose that's relevant?

                                                                    Re: Ergot?
  From: Chris Rutkowski <canadianuforeport.nul>
  Date: Mon, 20 Jul 2009 08:50:12 -0500
  Archived: Mon, 20 Jul 2009 11:05:00 -0400
  Subject: Re: Ergot?

  >From: Bruce Maccabee <brumac.nul>
  >Date: Sat, 18 Jul 2009 13:52:11 -0400
  >Archived: Sat, 18 Jul 2009 19:17:00 -0400
  >Subject: Ergot?
  >Has anyone come upon this word, ergot, in association with a UFO
  >abduction?
  Bruce et al:
  Yes, many years ago now, ergot was actually proposed as an
  explanation for abductees' experiences. Ergot contains a number
  of alkaloids which can cause physiological effects in animals
  and people. In fact, LSD was first synthesized from ergot.
  Outbreaks of ergot poisoning include an incident in France in
  the 1950s in which dozens of people had major hallucinations,
  thinking they were being attacked by tigers, leopards and other
  wild beasts, felt "burning" sensations on their bodies and saw
  glowing lights.
  It is not unexpected, then, that ergot poisoning was compared
  with abductee symptoms by some medical clinicians. I don't
  recall where I saw the first reference to link the two. It might
  have been a researcher named Nilsson, but that's all I recall.

  Chris Rutkowski

  Dear List:
  Years ago, went I began research into mind control and the
  possible link to the phenomena called abduction, Richard Hall
  supported my efforts to contact the National Institute of
  Health.
  Hall provided me, free of charge, booklets he had written on the
  nature of abductions. After reading his material, I decided he
  was a serious researcher attempting to provide the public with
  insights into the phenomena.
  As far as I am concerned, the man did it with grace and
  intelligence. I will forgive him for getting testy with me
  toward the end of his life (and on this List).
  Frankly, I find myself getting cranky every now and then as
  sometimes I feel I am dealing individuals who are having trouble
  just 'getting it'.

More Brazilian UFO Disclosure




                                                           UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Jul > Jul 20

                                                    More Brazilian UFO Disclosure
  From: A. J. Gevaerd - Revista UFO <aj.nul>
  Date: Mon, 20 Jul 2009 15:48:05 -0300
  Archived: Mon, 20 Jul 2009 17:32:00 -0400
  Subject: More Brazilian UFO Disclosure

  Dear Colleagues,
  It is a pleasure to tell you that the Brazilian UFO Disclosure
  is going better than expected.
  After two small and a medium size release of documents,
  respectively last year and last May 04, that I posted about
  here, we at the Brazilian Committee of UFO Researchers (CBU) -
  which promoted the campaign that resulted in this opening of
  files - have been officially informed that the government has
  now decided to disclose all documents produced in the 80s and
  kept secret so far. The release should be at the National
  Archives in 10 days.
  The Brazilian government has been gradually releasing a
  considerable amount of secret UFO documents, and doing so
  according to decade. So far we have material from the 60s and
  70s, most of these last ones released on May 04, specially 195
  pages and about 180 photos of Operation Saucer. Including
  previous leaks we now have over 500 pages of presumed 2,000
  pages on the investigation, conducted in the Amazon, by the
  Brazilian Air Force from September to December 1977. That is
  certainly the biggest _known_ official military investigation of
  UFOs in the world.
  So far, over 1,400 pages of Brazilian previously classified UFO
  documents have been released thanks to the efforts of the
  Brazilian UFO Researchers Committee (CBU), through the campaign
  UFOs: Freedom of Information Now, started in 2004 and
  articulated by the Brazilian UFO Magazine. This last one has
  been the biggest disclosure ever in Brazil, but the one expected
  for August should be _really_ big.
  This past weekend Brazilian UFO Disclosure finally reached the
  general public as the second largest circulation magazine in
  Brazil, ISTOE, published a nice cover and article of 8 pages
  dedicated to the opening of the files.
  No other general publication has devoted so much space to the
  subject so far. The reactions are considerable and the article
  is being re-published in hundreds of local papers and websites,
  potentializing the response from the people.
  See the article in PDF format here:
  www.ufo.com.br/public/istoe/Istoe_18_07_09.pdf
  As for the documents already released, last month I assembled a
  team of 30 volunteer translators to get at least the 500 pages
  already known (through the disclosures and previously leaks)
  into English for public release. It should be ready in a couple
  of weeks to be published here and elsewhere. We expect to get
  the rest of the papers, of other military missions, very soon
  too. It takes a lot of work.
  The PDF files of 12 envelopes or folders released last May can
  be can be downloaded from:
  www.ufo.com.br/public/abertura_2


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/jul/m20-011.shtml[11/12/2011 11:16:44]
More Brazilian UFO Disclosure


  Pay special attention to this one:
  Folder 10 - 1978, with 195 pages of Operation Saucer material,
  downloadable at:
  www.ufo.com.br/public/abertura_2/CENDOC_ENVELOPE_10_1978.pdf
  As said before, Brazilian UFO researchers are very excited about
  these new disclosures, that unquestionably prove that our
  efforts have been profitable for the entire Brazilian UFO
  Community. We also consider the Brazilian UFO Disclosure to be a
  solid, though painful response to the skepticism in Ufology in
  Brazil.
  Download more official disclosed documents at the Brazilian UFO
  Magazine FTP areas:
  www.ufo.com.br/public/abertura_1
  www.ufo.com.br/public/brasil
  www.ufo.com.br/public/documents
  www.ufo.com.br/public/prato
  Feel free to use and republish this material on your website,
  but please mention its origin as credit.

  A. J. Gevaerd,
  Editor, Brazilian UFO Magazine
  Coordinator, Brazilian UFO Researchers Committee
  www.ufo.com.br

  poisoning can therefore be caused by eating infected bread,
  which would affect a whole community almost immediately. There
  is a book about this by John Fuller (who we know, of course,
  from another context!) called The Day of St Anthony's Fire. Also
  check Wikipdia under 'St Anthony's Fire'.

  Stan,
  You more than anybody know who introduced the term MJ-12 (or
  whatever it has become now). Moore was suspicious enough to
  track down a name mentioned in a document called the Aquarius
  supposedly emanating from some group called MJ-12.
  Moore discovered that it was the code name for an over the
  horizon radar program. I cannot read the mind of Moore, but I
  know I would have red flags flying over the entire group of
  'documents' arriving under the name MJ-12.
  As I stated there was a working group - not secret, just not
  well known - but it was not called MJ-12 or anything remotely
  involving Ms and Js. It was the real group who reported to the
  President.
  Surely, you researched the name of the working group in
  government documents, just like I did. So, we both know that
  there was no research working group called MJ-12.
  I have no idea why, when this information is available, no one
  has challenged what has come to be simply a tradition within the
  UFO community. Yes, it means there is a gross problem with the
  Cantwheel material. Even you admitted to that, as I quote
  directly from you in my book.

  UFO researcher Richard Hall devoted over 50 years of his life to
  the understanding of the phenomenon. He, along with the likes of
  Donald Keyhoe, James McDonald, Alan Hynek, Stanton Friedman and
  others, was a legend in the field.
  They are part of the most extraordinary instance in history
  where citizen scientists and investigators worked to oppose the
  deliberate misrepresentation of reality by the State.
  Decade after decade they labored to get at the truth during a
  time (the Cold War) when the government of the United States was
  absolutely committed to the embargo of that truth from the
  public.
  PRG very much regrets that Mr. Hall did not live to see the
  lifting of that embargo and the vindication of his and his
  colleague's work in the eyes of the world. Nevertheless, the
  ongoing effort to the resolve the UFO/ET issue stands on the
  shoulders of these legends and could not succeed without their
  contributions.

We Come As Friends




                                                           UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Jul > Jul 21

                                                            We Come As Friends
  From: Katharina Wilson <K_Wilson.nul>
  Date: Tue, 21 Jul 2009 09:28:54 -0500
  Archived: Tue, 21 Jul 2009 14:43:00 -0400
  Subject: We Come As Friends

  Excerpted by Tom Boyle:
  From the book We Come As Friends, by Peter Michaels.
  P. 227
  -----
  A couple and their dog were on a boat out at sea and their boat
  started to sink. They sent out a distress call and an
  extraterrestrial beamed them all aboard the ET's craft. Here's
  an excerpt:
  ET : ..."I am very fond of your Earth dogs. They are quite
  amazing creatures. Rather unique personalities. Nothing quite
  like them anywhere else. I have taken several of them myself."
  Human: "So you saved us because you're a dog lover?"
  ET: "Yes. And I know enough to know that a dog that loses its
  masters will never be a happy dog again. If that were not the
  case, I might have just saved the dog."
  Human: "You mean you'd have let us die?" Chris said a bit
  angrily. "Even though you knew we were in trouble?"
  ET: "Yes. Don't have such a high opinion of yourselves," the
  creature said. "That is a very human trait. You mean nothing to
  me. I have a certain interest in your planet and visit it
  occasionally. But I have no concern over the fate of any given
  human beings. Consider yourselves lucky that your dog was with
  you, or else you might be dead by now."
  -----
  As an animal lover, I found this humorous. From the perspective
  of an abductee, this was most likely a true conversation.
  Lesson: Go to your local animal shelter and adopt a dog (or at
  least two cats) Who knows? The life you could be saving might be
  your own. :)

  Katharina

'Astronomers & UFOs' Argument - Again




                                                           UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Jul > Jul 22

                                            'Astronomers & UFOs' Argument - Again
  From: Chris Rutkowski <canadianuforeport.nul>
  Date: Tue, 21 Jul 2009 15:39:09 -0500
  Archived: Wed, 22 Jul 2009 14:39:58 -0400
  Subject: 'Astronomers & UFOs' Argument - Again

  In a recent post by skeptic Phil Plait (Bad Astronomer
  Extraordinaire), he once again brings up his argument that
  astronomers should be reporting most UFOs because they are the
  ones looking up in the sky, after all. Plait cites Tim Printy's
  recent SUNlite zine in which he debunks the astronomers/UFO myth
  in favour of Phil.
  [Aside, Tim is carrying on in the tradition of Phil Klass' SUN
  Newsletter which attacked ufology. His site is very good and
  highly recommended:
  http://home.comcast.net/~tprinty/UFO/SUNlite.htm)]
  Anyway, Phil makes the following statement:
  "I got lots of criticism from the UFO culture who, predictably,
  couldn’t parse my very simple logic. I got some amusement from
  it, I’ll admit, since trying to reason with some people is
  clearly a losing game."
  What great chutzpah! Phil thinks that no ufologist can
  understand his reasoning, therefore they are idiots.
  Plait argues that:
  "Astronomers, both amateur and professional, are constantly
  viewing the sky. There are tens of thousands of amateurs out
  observing all the time: a large sample population, and far
  larger in observing man-hours than the regular population. If
  UFOs are so common, then why do we not see an unusually large
  number of reports from astronomers?"
  He then answers his own question:
  "My assertion is that this is because the vast majority of UFO
  reports from people are misidentified objects like Venus, the
  Moon, satellites, balloons, and so on. These are things every
  amateur astronomer has seen countless times, and knows are not
  alien spaceships bent on probing the backsides of rural
  citizens. While this does not mean every single observed object
  is something more mundane, it does mean that the huge numbers
  quoted by UFOlogists are most certainly wrong."
  Printy comes to Plait's assistance in an article titled: "Why
  don’t astronomers see physical craft in the sky operating under
  intelligent control that defy explanation?"
  Of course, Printy's question is not the one that ufologists are
  asking, so there's a problem right from the start.
  But it's Plait's question that needs examination. The problem is
  that he's disconnected from UFO case research and doesn't know
  much about UFO data. Pointing out that astronomers do see UFOs
  does not rebut his argument at all, hence his assumption that
  ufologists are stupid. (Of course, a true scientist does not
  generalize or make assumptions.)
  Plait is actually asking why UFO databases are not overpopulated
  with reports from astronomers, since astronomers observe the sky


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/jul/m22-002.shtml[11/12/2011 11:16:49]
'Astronomers & UFOs' Argument - Again

  more than the average person. There are simple reasons for this.
  First of all, astronomers are unlikely to report UFO sightings
  to UFO investigators and researchers. The Canadian UFO Survey,
  for example, collected data from dozens of sources (including
  government and military sources), but very few of the reports
  were from astronomers. Really, would astronomers normally report
  their UFO sightings to UFO Updates? Therefore, most astronomers'
  UFO sightings do not get into UFO report databases.
  Astronomers are also unilkely to report anomalous objects to
  media. Instead, professional astronomical organizations are the
  norm. Comet and asteroid data are reported directlly to the IAU.
  Meteors and bolides are reported to MIAC and other specialized
  astronomy organizations. The average non-astronomer will report
  UFOs to either the media or to a UFO organization.
  And how many non-astronomers see UFOs? Plait suggests that the
  tens of thousands of amateur astronomers' observing hours far
  outweigh the bulk of non-astronomers' viewing time. One reply to
  Plait's blog points out that numbers don't addd up.
  Statistically, more non-astronomers are watching the skies than
  astronomers at any one time.
  Phil does parse the fact that most UFO reports are
  misidentifications. That's good. He then adds that amateur
  astronomers know what Venus and satellites look like and know
  they are not UFOs. Yes, but some astronomers report seeing
  things that are not easily-identifiable objects like Venus and
  satellites.
  And then Phil notes that somehow, "While this does not mean
  every single observed object is something more mundane, it does
  mean that the huge numbers quoted by UFOlogists are most
  certainly wrong."
  That statement simply doesn't make sense, parsing or no parsing.
  Huge numbers of what? UFO reports? It is factual that there are
  large numbers of reported UFOs on record. The files are
  available in many locations. The number of data points cannot be
  wrong. The interpretation can, but the number? No.
  It's an interesting discussion, at the very least. Good stuff.

  At:
  http://www.virtuallystrange.net/ufo/sdi/subscribers/

  Date: Thu, 23 Jul 2009 09:35:40 +1000

  Subject: Re: 'Astronomers & UFOs' Argument - Again

  >From: Chris Rutkowski <canadianuforeport.nul>

  >Subject: 'Astronomers & UFOs' Argument - Again

  >astronomers should be reporting most UFOs because they are the

  >in favour of Phil.
  Hi Chris,
  I posted about this yesterday on the Daily Grail:
  http://dailygrail.com/news/astronomers-and-ufos-one-more-time
  quoting from your good self on a number of occasions.
  I think there are three key problems in the 'setup' here:
  a) The definition of "UFO". In his Bad Astronomy book, Phil
  mentions it in the context of "I have never heard about any
  amateur astronomers seeing something in the sky they absolutely
  could not explain". At various other times, Phil gets a little
  lazy and just uses it without defining it, in which case it's
  simply read as "unidentified flying object". Several rounds
  later, we have Tim Printy defining UFOs - in the context of
  criticisms of Phil's opinion - as "physical objects that are
  actual 'craft' operating under 'intelligent control' that defy
  explanation", or later "immensely large “aerial vehicles”
  hovering over their observing site."
  No wonder that we find it hard to agree on how many UFOs are
  being reported by amateur astronomers!
  b) The definition of "ufologist": Phil seems to think that the
  term refers to any nutcase who emails him dribbling about an
  alien conspiracy. My definition is very different.
  Misidentifying a group, and generalising about that group based
  on a small subset, is always going to lead to criticisms. Which
  is what Phil got.
  c) The fallacious claim that "ufologists" think all UFO
  sightings are ET craft:
  This follows on from (b) somewhat - if I identify my kids as
  "amateur astronomers", based on the fact that they like to sit
  outside and look at the night sky, then I could say with some
  confidence that "amateur astronomers believe that some sky
  monster must have taken a bite out of the Moon".
  As you've said yourself though, "no serious ufologist believes
  that the majority of reported UFOs are flying saucers.... It's
  nice to see that Phil has arrived at the same view as we have,
  only 40 or 50 years behind."



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/jul/m22-003.shtml[11/12/2011 11:16:49]
Re: 'Astronomers & UFOs' Argument - Again

  d) Phil's anecdote versus documented evidence: The Bad
  Astronomer is keen to dismiss UFO anecdote, but thinks that his
  personal anecdote about amateur astronomers never approaching
  him with UFO reports (I wonder why, duh?) shows that they don't.
  He doesn't reference at all actual surveys that have been done,
  such as the Gert Herb report, which show that amateur
  astronomers have reported strange things, objects observed "at
  short enough distance as to leave no doubt in the observer’s
  mind that something strange was reported."

  Kind regards,
  Greg

Shag Harbour Rip Dive Report - July 20 2009




                                                           UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena

                                         Shag Harbour Rip Dive Report - July 20 2009
  From: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  Date: Wed, 22 Jul 2009 20:33:02 -0400
  Archived: Wed, 22 Jul 2009 20:33:02 -0400
  Subject: Shag Harbour Rip Dive Report - July 20 2009



  David Cvet, former Torontonian & MUFON Ontario member, sent me
  the following, today, via Facebook:
  -----
  For the past two years, I've been attempting a dive expedition
  off Shag Harbour, in an area known as the Shag Harbour Rip, in
  which anomalous sonar readings were made by a survey ship called
  the Navicula back in 1988 for the Bedford Institute of
  Oceanography.
  These anomalous sonar readings happen to reside on or near the
  surmised path that the "dark object" took when it departed Shag
  Harbour and traveled north to Shelburne underwater. These sonar
  targets deserved closer examination.
  Over the past couple of years, the planning had been hampered by
  poor weather, rough water and hurricanes. However, this past
  Monday, weather cooperated, and allowed us to conduct the first
  preliminary dive in that area. I have written a full report with
  photos located at: www.cuun.ca/shag_harbour_dive_jul_2009.php
  -----
  Below is the text from David's report...
  ebk
  -----

  Source: Canadian UAO-USO Network
  http://www.cuun.ca/shag_harbour_dive_jul_2009.php
  July 20, 2009

  Shag Harbour Rip Dive Report - July 20, 2009
  Introduction
  The events and evidence surrounding the Shag Harbour incident of
  October 4, 1967 are compelling, resulting in a number of
  individuls who actively continue research into this incident,
  and who are uncovering more intriguing evidence and facts in
  support of this extraordinary event. This dive expedition
  organized by David M. Cvet, is another research angle
  contributing to the growing data and evidence collected
  supporting this incident. The purpose of the dive expedition was
  two-fold:
  * firstly, to gather intelligence on the water, sea bed and
  diving conditions in an area south-west tip off of Outer Island,
  in an area called the "Shag Harbour Rip", and
  * secondly, to attempt to qualify the anomalous sonar targets


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/jul/m22-004.shtml[11/12/2011 11:16:50]
Shag Harbour Rip Dive Report - July 20 2009

  (readings) obtained by the ship Navicula [1], which were
  identified as "may not be boulders" during their bottom survey
  in 1988 located at the following coordinates: 43 degrees 26
  minutes 10 seconds N by 65 degrees 46 minutes 15 seconds W.
  The targets were described as four depressions or mounds on the
  sea bed, which were differentiated enough from the surrounding
  sea bed causing these to be highlighted on the sonar reading
  taken. The reason for the interest in these sonar targets is
  that they happen to reside on or near what may have been the
  path on which the "dark object" had traversed out of Shag
  Harbour on that evening of October 4, 1967 which then turned
  northward traveling about 27km towards Shelburne.
  The Expedition Planning and Execution
  Expedition Team: David M. Cvet (diver & lead), Laurie McGowan
  (diver, underwater photographer), Freeland Reynolds (diver,
  remained on surface to assist, and operate the communications
  radio, surface-to-diver), Rick Davis (videographer).
  Numerous attempts were made to organize this preliminary dive
  over the past two years, however, the dive never materialized
  due to it being hampered by a number of reasons including:
  unavailability of an appropriate boat, diver/people
  availability, uncooperative weather conditions (hurricanes,
  storms, rough waters). The planning was repeatedly plagued with
  one or more of aforementioned situations until Monday, July 20.
  The Expedition Planning and Execution
  Expedition Team: David M. Cvet (diver & lead), Laurie McGowan
  (diver, underwater photographer), Freeland Reynolds (diver,
  remained on surface to assist, and operate the communications
  radio, surface-to-diver), Rick Davis (videographer).
  Numerous attempts were made to organize this preliminary dive
  over the past two years, however, the dive never materialized
  due to it being hampered by a number of reasons including:
  unavailability of an appropriate boat, diver/people
  availability, uncooperative weather conditions (hurricanes,
  storms, rough waters). The planning was repeatedly plagued with
  one or more of aforementioned situations until Monday, July 20.
  Having contacted Timothy Gillespie in Shelburne, he forwarded
  contact information of an individual by the name of Stephen
  Goreham, who had access to a 45ft swordfishiing boat. Confirming
  the availability of the boat on the Friday prior to the dive day
  on Monday, the swordfishing boat became unavailable, however, he
  managed to obtain the services of a 45ft lobster boat by the
  name of "The Receiver General", captained by Vincent Goreham.
  The planned dive was to be around 2pm Monday, at the point of
  low tide during slack tide, thus reducing the depth of the dive,
  and therefore, increasing bottom time. Diver Laurie McGowan out
  of Annapolis Royal confirmed, as did Freeland Reynolds (he did
  not dive and remained on the surface to help, and operate the
  radio). Videographer Rick Davis also confirmed, so a base team
  was formed for the dive expedition on the Monday.
  The dive profile was based on the expectation that the bottom
  would be approximately 110 - 120 ft in depth. The dive plan was
  to enter the water, descend to approximately 80 ft and conduct a
  final equipment and personnel check before proceeding to the
  bottom. Bottom time at that depth, using standard dive tanks
  allowed for 10 minutes. This was so that decompression can be
  avoided. Multi-tank dives to lengthen bottom time, would require
  a signifant additional equipment to allow for decompression, a
  boat equipped to handle decompression in the water, and access
  to a decompression chamber on-shore should problems arise. This
  would significantly complicate the dive, and increase the risk
  factor. It was felt that this preliminary dive's objectives can
  be satisfied within the allowed time frame. However, when we
  arrived at our destination, the depth turned out to be only 70-
  80 ft. This would allow for 20-30 minutes bottom time.
  The team arrived at Lower Woods Harbour was around 12:30pm. The
  equipment was loaded onto the deck of the boat, and shoved off
  just before 1pm. The trip to the destination took about 30
  minutes. Upon arrival at the coordinates of the alleged sonar
  anomalous targets, a marker bouy was dropped to mark the


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/jul/m22-004.shtml[11/12/2011 11:16:50]
Shag Harbour Rip Dive Report - July 20 2009

  position. While in transit to the destination, the dive
  equipment were prepared and assembled. The weather was perfect
  for diving, clear sky, calm waters (rollers approx 1m), slight
  breeze, surface temperature approx 22C).
  Both divers' equipment were standard, comprised of the usual
  dry/wet suit, 80 cu.ft. tanks, regs, BC, etc. David wore a full
  face-mask which is fitted with a radio transmitter [2], and the
  receiver and transmitter was on the surface operated by
  Freeland. Laurie was to operate the underwater camera which was
  on loan to him from David (Nikon D80 in an Ikelite camera
  housing). Some minor technical problems with David's BC, where
  the valve to control the inflation of the BC was siezed. Using
  an adjustable C-wrench, the valve was removed, and the
  components were doused with a WD-40 like compound, and it fixed
  the problem.

  The Water
  The divers entered the water upcurrent from the marker and
  descended. To the surprise of both divers, the water was heavily
  clouded greenish/brownish suspension allowing visibility of no
  more than 2m. At approximately 40ft, David was experiencing some
  equalization issues with his left ear (this is not entirely
  unusal due to sinus problems for the past 25 years). Once
  equalization was obtained, both divers continued towards the
  bottom.
  Visibility did not improve at the bottom, and the current,
  although slight was noticeable and was flowing in the same
  direction as the divers towards the marker bouy. The bottom
  temperature was recorded as 15C. The ambiant light was lower
  than expected due to the suspension in the water creating a
  challenge for photographing. The water was described as being
  like "pea soup".
  The divers proceeded towards the marker bouy using compasses to
  guide them. The bottom texture was observed to be pebbly,
  comprised of random sized pebbles (smoothed, roughly spherical
  or elliptical shaped stones) from 4 to 20 cm in size. The bottom
  was also strewn with random sized irregular shaped rocks as well
  typically larger than the largest pebbles. Between the pebbles
  and rocks was a silty sediment, easily stirred by the action of
  our fins. The bottom was populated with a variety of flora and
  fauna growing on and between the rocks. Scallops and lobsters
  were abundant. The divers were swimming literally inches off the
  bottom as visibilty was extremely poor, and depth perception was
  difficult at best.
  Enroute towards the marker, the divers came across what appeared
  to be a slight depression. This depression was easily noticed
  because it was obviously different from the surrounding texture
  of the bottom. The depression was gentle and appeared to have a
  diameter of at least 15 - 20ft. The depression's bottom was
  comprised of more or less regularly shaped pebbles approximately
  4-6cm in size. This was inconsistent with the surrounding area
  of essentially randomly sized rocks and pebbles. A second
  observation made was that there was no flora attached or growing
  in the depression. It was as if it was cleared of any debris and
  vegetation, again, inconsistent with the surrounding sea bed.
  After a few minutes of examination, the divers continued towards
  the marker, and shortly after came across a second depression,
  sharing the attributes of the first depression.
  It is possible that the sonar on the survey ship picked up these
  depressions and highlighted these on its readings because of
  their visible difference from the surrounding sea bed. It was
  possible that the divers had stumbled onto two of the four
  depressions (sonar anomalies) noted on the sonar target data.
  However, given the extremely poor visibility, the divers did not
  search for the other two depressions so as to confirm and map to
  the original sonar reading of four targets.
  The bottom time was approximately 25 minutes and the divers
  surfaced and boarded the boat concluding the formal dive.
  Final impressions and conclusions
  The dive approach whereby, the divers manually search the bottom


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/jul/m22-004.shtml[11/12/2011 11:16:50]
Shag Harbour Rip Dive Report - July 20 2009

  for the sonar targets was extremely challenged by the poor
  visibility in the water. The water condition may have been
  degraded with the unusual amount of rain on NS during June and
  July, and therefore, causing an increase in sediment and
  particulate suspension. It was suggested that should a repeat
  dive be planned, that an alternative season (fall/winter) be
  considered with the hope that the waters would be clearer,
  however, fall/winter weather conditions are even less
  predictable, and is during the hurricane season.
  It was thought that the best approach would be to have highly
  sensitive equipment such as a bottom profiler, multi-frequency
  side-scanner and magnetometers to more accurately locate the
  sonar targets, mark it with a marker bouy, and then have divers
  descend to the target. This would help with removing any
  uncertainties improve the timing for the location of the
  depressions. It would be more valuable to be able to locate all
  four depressions and map them to the anomalous targets thus
  proving that the depressions were the cause of the anomalous
  readings in 1988.
  The lack of flora on the depression bottom was observed and
  thought unusual given the surrounding area was populated with
  flora and fauna. Given that there is thought that this may be
  related to the incident reported in Shag Harbour, it would be
  useful to have dosimeters and other devices to determine if
  there were any usual characteristics of the depressions.
  Gathering samples of the pebbles would also be useful to
  continue further examination on the surface. Also, plan for
  escavating the depression to determine if there is something
  unusual beneath the depression.
  The dive was considered a success, as much data was garnered
  during the dive in terms of water, bottom and dive conditions,
  useful for planning future dive expeditions. The discovery of
  the depressions, which may or may not play the part of the
  original sonar anomalies was a bonus, but, given only two were
  observed, the outcome remains inconclusive. The desire is to
  repeat the dive, but with additional equipment and
  instrumentation, and scheduled during late winter or early
  spring.
  Footnotes:
  1. The Navicula is a survey ship, operating out of the Bedford
  Institute of Oceanography or BIO, in Dartmouth, NS. In 1988, it
  was tasked to conduct a survey of the sea bed. Officially, the
  report is recorded as: "Cruise Report 88-018 [B] Phase Ii; 88-
  018[b] Phase Iii, F.r.v. Navicula, Yarmouth, South - Yarmouth,
  North, June 5th - June 17th, 1988", authored by Miller, R O and
  Fader, G B J. Report released in December, 1989.
  2. Most divers wear diving goggles, and bite on a regulator for
  their source of air. A full face-mask configuration has a
  connector to the air source, and the entire face is within the
  mask. The mask has a "cowl" like structure, similar to what
  fighter pilots wear on their face. Communications is included in
  the mask, allowing the diver to talk to the surface by
  depressing a transmit button on the face-mask.
  Selected photos from the expedition (all photos by Laurie
  McGowan)
  [At site]
  David M. Cvet
  C=B7U=B7U=B7N

                                                           Another Salla Moment
  From: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  Date: Wed, 22 Jul 2009 21:35:00 -0400
  Archived: Wed, 22 Jul 2009 21:35:00 -0400
  Subject: Another Salla Moment



  In the light of Michael Salla's continuing to grasp at straws in the
  sky - Bill Knell being the latest - and the passing of Richard Hall,
  perhaps its appropriate to re-visit a blog-post of Paul Kimball's...
  With thanks to 'The Norm' for the memory-jog,
  ebk
  -----
  Source: Paul Kimball's Blog - Halifax, Nova Scotia, Canada
  http://tinyurl.com/l35hfs
  Sunday, August 21, 2005

  Michael Salla's 'Dan Quayle' Moment
  One should always be careful about comparing oneself to an icon.
  For example, former Vice-President Dan Quayle may be best
  remembered for his 1988 debate with Democratic VP candidate,
  Senator Lloyd Bentsen.

  Quayle, who was perceived as a lightweight who did not have the
  experience to be President, should the need arise, compared his
  length of experience with that of John Kennedy.
  Bentsen, in what was one of the most devastating political
  exchanges in American history, pounced:
  "Senator, I served with Jack Kennedy. I knew Jack Kennedy. Jack
  Kennedy was a friend of mine. Senator, you're no Jack Kennedy."
  Quayle tried to answer:
  "That remark was uncalled for, Senator."
  Bentsen twisted the dagger:
  "You're the one making the comparison, Senator... Frankly, I
  think you're so far apart in the objectives you choose for your
  country that I did not think the comparison was well taken."
  And that was that.
  Fortunately for the Republicans, people vote for the top of the
  ticket, so George Bush survived his selection of Quayle as his
  running-mate, but Quayle never recovered from the impression
  created by that one moment. It defined him. [For anyone
  interested, the full debate transcript can be found at:
  www.debates.org/pages/trans88c.html
  I call this a "Dan Quayle" moment.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/jul/m22-005.shtml[11/12/2011 11:16:51]
Another Salla Moment


  Exopolitics-guru Dr. Michael Salla has just had one.
  It all began when Dr. Salla, who was getting rhetorically and
  logically pounded by Brad Sparks, Kevin Randle, Stan Friedman
  and others at UFO Updates, sought to invoke the memory of Major
  Donald Keyhoe, one of ufology's early pioneers, by claiming that
  Keyhoe was, in fact, the idealogical father of exopolitics. When
  some suggested that this was "a bit much" (to be polite), Salla
  reiterated the comparison.
  That was a mistake.
  Dick Hall, playing the role of Lloyd Bentsen, has now weighed in
  on Salla's attempt to compare himself to Keyhoe (which, in
  ufological circles, is sort of like comparing yourself to
  Kennedy in politics).
  Here's what Dick had to say at UFO Updates today, in response to
  Salla (who had the effrontery to call Dick - and by implication
  anyone else who disagreed with Salla - "naive"):
  "Michael,
  I will venture to say without fear of contradiction that I knew
  and understood Major Keyhoe and his thinking far better than you
  did or ever will. This will be my last response to anything you
  post, as you have no concept of logic, reason, or evidence and
  it is a total waste of time trying to communicate with you.
  The final sentence of your first paragraph quoted above about
  intelligence agencies quashing UFO research, removing evidence,
  etc. is pure and simple nonsense. Nothing but bald assumption
  followed by circular reasoning. Some specific evidence can be
  hidden at least temporarily, but evidence trails cannot be
  removed completely as several of your dubious sources claim.
  Major Keyhoe never believed what you say. We all thought UFOs
  probably were spaceships; that's no secret. But Maj. Keyhoe
  always depicted two opposing camps within the Air Force, one of
  which favored total secrecy and one that wanted the full truth
  to come out. This analysis has been totally confirmed by
  historical documentation, and is a far cry from what you are
  claiming. Later he came to believe that the CIA was behind the
  secrecy policy, and there also is good evidence of that being
  true after 1952
  You go right ahead and spout your exocranial blatherings. No
  real scholar is going to pay any attention to them, and as Paul
  Kimball pointed out, the dubious and often badly flawed
  "sources" that you continually cite will go a long way toward
  further discrediting the subject in the eyes of the people whose
  help we badly need."
  - Dick" [The full post can be found at:
  www.virtuallystrange.net/ufo/updates/2005/aug/m21-005.shtml
  Allow me to translate:
  Dick - "Dr. Salla, I served with Donald Keyhoe. I knew Donald
  Keyhoe. Donald Keyhoe was a friend of mine. Dr. Salla, you're no
  Donald Keyhoe."
  Salla (as I anticipate his response will be) - "That remark was
  uncalled for, Mr. Hall."
  Dick - "You're the one making the comparison, Dr. Salla.
  Frankly, I think you're so apart in the objectives that you
  choose for ufology that I did not think the comparison was well
  taken."
  Exactly.
  The only difference between the 1988 vice-presidential debate
  and the 2005 ufological debate is that, unlike Quayle and
  Bentsen, Hall and Salla are pretty much at the top of their
  respective "tickets."
  It's time for ufology to choose.



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/jul/m22-005.shtml[11/12/2011 11:16:51]
Another Salla Moment

  The UFO Evidence vs. Exopolitics.
  The Real Inheritor of Keyhoe's legacy vs. The Imposter.
  Fact vs. Fiction.
  I know where my vote goes.

  Paul Kimball

                                    Apollo Or Extinction - Clarke On Space Exploration
  From: Diana Cammack <cammack.nul>
  Date: Thu, 23 Jul 2009 19:29:18 +0200
  Archived: Thu, 23 Jul 2009 20:42:33 -0400
  Subject: Apollo Or Extinction - Clarke On Space Exploration

  http://www.truthout.org/072309C?n
  Thursday 23 July 2009
  Apollo or Extinction
  by: Tad Daley, t r u t h o u t | Perspective
  Monday marked the 40th anniversary of the Apollo 11 landing on
  the moon.
  The existential goal of space travel is human immortality.
  On December 31, 1999, National Public Radio interviewed the
  futurist and science fiction genius Arthur C. Clarke. Since the
  author had forecast so many of the 20th century's most
  fundamental developments, the NPR correspondent asked Clarke if
  anything had happened in the preceding 100 years that he never
  could have anticipated. "Yes, absolutely," Clarke replied,
  without a moment's hesitation. "The one thing I never would have
  expected is that, after centuries of wonder and imagination and
  aspiration, we would have gone to the moon... and then
  stopped."
  Were Clarke alive today, he undoubtedly would have added, "and
  then lost so much interest that we erased the tapes of our
  epochal voyage because of a shortage of blank cassettes."
  This month, the 40th anniversary of Buzz Aldrin and Neil
  Armstrong's first footsteps on the moon, you will hear many
  rationales for sending humans into space, many noble goals that
  the challenge of space can help humanity to fulfill. However, in
  cosmological consequence, one, and only one, stands paramount
  above all others - human immortality. Space is the only place
  where we can ensure ourselves against extinction.
  Jonathan Schell, our great chronicler of the dilemmas of the
  nuclear age, has written often about the ascending gradations of
  extermination that human beings might commit. Genocide is an act
  aimed at annihilating all members of a particular human group -
   defined by ethnicity or religion or some other perceived
  collective hatred - Hitler's attempt to obliterate the Jews the
  most famous but hardly the only historical example. Specicide
  would be an act eliminating the whole of the human race.
  Ecocide, or perhaps biocide, or perhaps omnicide, would be an
  act exterminating not just all humans, but the entire circle of
  life on planet earth itself.
  An asteroid impact, or certain kinds of disruptions of our sun,
  or perhaps other cosmological cataclysms could probably pull all
  those off without even breaking a sweat. And our sun, in any
  case, has an expiration date some 4 or 5 billion years down the
  road. A quarter century before the voyages of Apollo, the
  invention of the nuclear weapon gave life on Earth, for the
  first time, the capacity to bring about its own extinction by
  its own hands. It will not be long before biotech and nanotech
  and god-knows-what-other techs obtain the same capacity. And it
  is far from impossible to suppose that human-induced climate
  change may unfold so badly in the decades to come that it, too,
  could threaten to bring about the same result.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/jul/m23-002.shtml[11/12/2011 11:16:52]
Apollo Or Extinction - Clarke On Space Exploration


  This period, where we hold this capability to destroy ourselves
  but before we have found a way to save ourselves, might be
  called the human race's ultimate "window of vulnerability." But
  we also now possess a "window of opportunity," to endeavor, in,
  oh, the next five or ten centuries or so, to establish the human
  race permanently beyond the cradle of its birth - first perhaps
  on our moon, then perhaps on Mars and in the asteroid belt and
  on some of the moons of Jupiter and Saturn and then beyond the
  bounds of our solar system itself. We have it within our grasp
  to venture slowly but inexorably outward, in tiny lifeboats
  afloat on an infinite sea, to explore and then to colonize and
  then to live our lives among the stars. Imagine our galaxy, a
  mere thousand years hence, with millions of homo sapiens who are
  born, who live and who die without ever setting foot on planet
  earth. Once we achieve that, once we have indeed established an
  enduring and self-sustaining human presence off the planet of
  our origin, it becomes very difficult to envision any
  comprehensive catastrophe that could eliminate completely the
  progeny of Mother Earth. Then, it would seem, we would be as
  close to immortality as the universe itself.
  Does this mean we should not devote extraordinary efforts to the
  prevention of extinction-inducing catastrophes here on earth? Of
  course not. We should abolish nuclear weapons and endeavor to
  enact a universal, verifiable and enforceable Nuclear Weapons
  Elimination Convention. We should impose serious transnational
  regulatory constraints on biotech and nanotech, before the
  development and proliferation of those technologies makes it
  much more difficult to do so. We should dedicate the kinds of
  resources and attentions to ameliorating the worst effects of
  climate change that the magnitude of the stakes requires. And we
  should even invest seriously in things like the monitoring of
  celestial bodies - so that, with perhaps decades of warning
  about an imminent collision between a big space rock and
  ourselves, we might figure out a way to avoid it.
  Nevertheless, even people who eat right, exercise every day and
  look both ways before crossing Fifth Avenue still take out
  policies for life insurance.
  Stephen Hawking, just before boarding his zero gravity airplane
  flight in April 2007, said, "Life on Earth is at risk of being
  wiped out by a disaster, such as sudden global warming, nuclear
  war, a genetically engineered virus.... I think the human race
  has no future if it doesn't go into space." Clarke, speaking
  from Sri Lanka on a huge video screen to aficionados gathered in
  Kansas City to commemorate the centennial on 7/7/7, of his
  fellow science fiction giant, the late Robert A. Heinlein, said
  "I have no doubt that the great master will be revered by future
  generations - if any." Heinlein himself, some years earlier,
  said that the actions of the thousands who worked to lift a
  fragile spacecraft off the surface of the earth and set it down
  gently on the surface of the moon "tend toward the survival of
  the entire race of mankind. The door they opened leads to hope
  that h. sapiens will survive indefinitely long, even longer than
  this solid planet on which we stand tonight. As a direct result
  of what they did, it is now possible that the human race will
  never die."
  But not if we wipe ourselves out before we have scarcely even
  gotten started. Not if we fail to dodge the bullet in the
  chamber in the gun in our own hands. Not if we lose so much
  interest in the fate of humankind that we erase not just the
  tapes of our most epochal achievements, but wind up erasing
  ourselves.
  Col. Ilan Ramon was the first-ever Israeli astronaut, who
  perished aboard the space shuttle Columbia in February 2003.
  When he was training for his flight, he contacted officials at
  Yad Vashem, the Holocaust Martyrs' and Heroes' Remembrance
  Authority in Jerusalem, and asked if he might be allowed to
  carry along with him a relic of the Holocaust. Yad Vashem
  officials searched deeply through their archives - and then
  suddenly came across the only possible answer to Colonel Ramon's
  request. They provided him with an exact replica of a small
  pencil sketch of the landscape of the moon, with our beautiful
  earth looming above the far horizon, lonely and fragile and
  whole, suspended among the blazing stars. It was created by a
  14-year-old Jewish boy named Petr Ginz. He drew it in 1942, more
  than a quarter century before the Apollo 8 astronauts became the


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/jul/m23-002.shtml[11/12/2011 11:16:52]
Apollo Or Extinction - Clarke On Space Exploration

  first humans to witness that whole earth, while incarcerated
  behind the walls of the Theresienstadt ghetto.
  Two years later, Petr Ginz was murdered behind the walls of
  Auschwitz.
  For that boy to sketch that sight, so long before any human had
  actually seen that sight and in the midst of the greatest
  degradation of the human spirit imaginable, can only be called a
  triumph of the imagination and a triumph of the will. Petr Ginz
  did not survive the great holocaust of his era. Ilan Ramon did
  not survive his journey into the cosmos. But if we aspire now to
  equal the imagination and the will of Petr Ginz, and if we
  endeavor now to complete the mission on which Ilan Ramon
  embarked, then - even if another great holocaust comes and even
  if despite all our struggles it brings to our fair planet not
  just genocide but specicide or omnicide - we still can.
  --------
  Tad Daley is the writing fellow with International Physicians
  for the Prevention of Nuclear War, the Nobel Peace Laureate
  organization. His first book, "APOCALYPSE NEVER: Forging the
  Path to a Nuclear Weapon-Free World," is forthcoming in January
  2010 from Rutgers University Press. .

  trace remnant from an alleged UFO landing back in 1884 in
  Benkelman, Nebraska.
  http://www.mccookgazette.com/story/1556905.html

  once analyzed but what's extra special is that it's from an
  incident from 1884. Who knows what else is lying around in
  vaults and as I once joked years ago that we do have physical
  evidence from UFO cases that some people use as paperweights,
  doorstops, and jewelry.
  Anyone know of this Benkleman, Nebraska UFO story from 1884?

                                     Re: 1884 UFO Landing Left Green Glass Residue?
  From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
  Date: Fri, 24 Jul 2009 20:09:33 -0400
  Archived: Fri, 24 Jul 2009 09:50:00 -0400
  Subject: Re: 1884 UFO Landing Left Green Glass Residue?

  >From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  >To: post.nul, sightings.nul
  >Date: Thu, 23 Jul 2009 15:58:22 EDT
  >Subject: 1884 UFO Landing Left Green Glass Residue?

  >Here's something you don't see everyday, some reported physical
  >trace remnant from an alleged UFO landing back in 1884 in
  >Benkelman, Nebraska.
  >http://www.mccookgazette.com/story/1556905.html
  >We'll see what the large piece of green glass like substance is
  >once analyzed but what's extra special is that it's from an
  >incident from 1884. Who knows what else is lying around in
  >vaults and as I once joked years ago that we do have physical
  >evidence from UFO cases that some people use as paperweights,
  >doorstops, and jewelry.
  >Anyone know of this Benkleman, Nebraska, UFO story from 1884?

  Having investigated this story some 25 years ago, I can lay this
  one quickly to rest. Well at least for a while; we may safely
  presume it will be back at some point. I have written about it
  both in an IUR article (November/December 1986 issue) and in my
  UFO Encyclopedia (2nd ed., pp. 259-60). To dispose of this
  canard as quickly as possible:
  I contacted, among others, the head of the Dundy County
  Historical Society, who had lived in the area all of her long
  life, and the well-known Nebraska folklorist/historian Roger
  Welsch.
  The former, who had researched the story years after her big-
  city journalist son learned of it, interviewed old-timers who
  knew the rancher (a real guy) mentioned in the newspaper
  account.
  This was all _before_ I came upon the follow-up report which
  alleged that during a subsequent rainstorm the remains of the
  Martian ship dissolved into salt, presumably the substance with
  which we are to take the yarn concerning them.
  Suffice it to say, like many other dubious tales from the
  period, it never happened. It's just another frontier tall tale
  and newspaper hoax, one of many preserved in America's
  freewheeling Western press.

  Jerry Clark

                                              Gibbs Asked About UFOs On C-SPAN
  From: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  Date: Fri, 24 Jul 2009 15:04:50 -0400
  Archived: Fri, 24 Jul 2009 15:04:50 -0400
  Subject: Gibbs Asked About UFOs On C-SPAN



  Source: The Huffington Post
  http://tinyurl.com/l3chuz
  07-24-09

  Gibbs Asked About UFOs On C-SPAN
  This morning on C-SPAN, White House Press Secretary Gibbs
  fielded an unusual question about goverment transparency. The
  questioner wasn't concerned with CIA reports or white house
  visitor logs. The latest alleged cover-up? UFOs.
  "My question might seem a little bit silly but it's very serious
  and I was wondering if you could take it very seriously." Ryan
  from Seattle said. "Let the American people know the truth about
  UFOs..."
  With a hint of a smile Gibbs responded. "Well Ryan I take your
  question seriously, not simply because of the topic but also
  because if you're calling from Seattle and it's a little after 7
  here... it's a little after 4 in the morning there." Gibbs
  paused. "Look... I have not been briefed on whether or not there
  are... the existence of UFOs, I haven't talked to the President
  about this topic."

  [Video at site]

                                                                UFO The Movie!
  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  Date: Fri, 24 Jul 2009 20:58:48 EDT
  Archived: Sat, 25 Jul 2009 05:59:40 -0400
  Subject: UFO The Movie!

  http://scifiwire.com/2009/07/feature-based-on-anderson-2.php
  I remember this show, UFO by the great Gerry Anderson.
  We who grew up in the 60's and 70's loved his special effects,
  stories, and of course those kickin' theme songs.
  One of his most controversial shows was titled UFO.
  It was about a secret group called SHADO that defended the Earth
  from body part stealing aliens.
  Like most sci-fi from that era it was full of fighting and space
  girls in go go boots and mini skirts. Heck, the latest Star Trek
  movie had girls in go go boots and mini skirts.
  Go figure.
  Well it's getting it's feature movie on the way and sure to be
  something to watch and be a conversation piece for this field of
  research.

  Best,
  Greg
